## [2026-07-28] tooling | Built `edgelab/` — a clean, isolated research+validation FRAMEWORK (not another one-off script). Packages: research (arXiv q-fin scraper, no key, rate-limited + abstract scoring + candidate-fiche extraction that separates article-claims from our reformulation), data (DataProvider ABC -> MT5-csv/Yahoo, swappable), edges (BaseEdge + 3 families: ts-momentum/z-mean-reversion/vol-breakout), backtest (event-driven, signal@t->fill@open t+1 no-lookahead, spread+slippage both sides, walk-forward+embargo, full metrics), risk (mandatory SL+TP+time-exit per trade, first-touch pessimistic ties; PropFirmRules: daily-loss lock / total-DD fail / profit-target / min-days / consistency -> PASSED/FAILED/IN_PROGRESS), portfolio (daily-return corr -> greedy decorrelated selection |rho|<0.3 -> equal/risk-parity combine -> book-level prop re-check). 22 unit tests pass (risk engine, drawdown, no-lookahead). | End-to-end run on MT5 D1 x 9 prop assets REPRODUCES the wiki thesis honestly: naive single-asset edges are marginal/negative net of cost (ts-mom Sharpe -0.03, mean-rev -1.17 FAILED, vol-breakout +0.22 IN_PROGRESS) -> only vol-breakout clears Sharpe>=0, so the "decorrelated portfolio" is 1 edge. Framework's value = it MEASURES and REJECTS, matching exp-001/003/006. Real edges still need a structurally different (cross-sectional/breadth) signal, not more OHLC variants. Entry pts: `python -m edgelab.run_pipeline`, `python -m edgelab.run_research`. README + config.yaml carry all params.
## [2026-07-28] research+experiment | User asked to arXiv-search 28 intraday topics (ORB/opening-price/microstructure/order-flow/HMM/regime/lead-lag/PEAD) + find an intraday edge with SL/TP, perf in R (SL=-1R). arXiv: 106 papers pulled via edgelab.research (exact-phrase q-fin gave 7/28 topics -> ORB-type terms are practitioner, thin in academia; relaxed keyword-AND recovered the real academic edges: intraday momentum, order-flow imbalance, HMM intraday momentum, PEAD, HF lead-lag, regime-switching, DeepScalper RL). Built edgelab/intraday/orb.py: session-aware (ET, DST-safe) Opening Range Breakout, R=entry-to-stop so SL=-1R, per-bar MT5 `spread` cost (medians match exp-005: US30 26pt, NAS100 13-15pt), no-lookahead (break on close -> fill next open). | VERDICT NEGATIVE (honest, robust): swept ORB OR{10,15,30,60}xTP{1,2,3R}+hold-to-close x{long,short,both} x vol-regime-filter x 6 assets + Market Intraday Momentum. ALL breakeven-to-negative net of spread: NAS100 ORB best E[R] -0.012 PF0.98, MIM -0.061 PF0.93; the few PF~1.03 cases (NAS100-short/XAU-long drift) are lumpy (pos 2-3/9yrs). Energies destroyed (spread>>range, cost>1R). Reproduces exp-001/exp-005: on demo M10 the ORB is breakeven; the thin real PF1.2-1.3 needs REAL ticks + vendor-tuned EA. M10 PF 1.15 E[R]+0.072 maxDD 18R pos 7/9yrs; confirmed on freshly-pulled real M1 (3.0M bars): PF 1.07-1.10 E[R]+0.04R maxDD ~20R pos 7/9yrs (only 2018 neg). Long-only (shorts fight NAS uptrend). Reproduces exp-005's NAS100 US-open brick via clean textbook ORB, in R with SL=-1R. NOT a new brick (same family/asset as exp-005). XAU/US30 marginally +; silver/crude/natgas untradeable (spread>>range). Tools: edgelab/data/mt5_live.py (live pull w/ per-bar spread), edgelab/intraday/orb.py (tz-correct), NAS100_M1.parquet cached.

## [2026-07-28] research-crosscheck | HONEST CORRECTION: earlier backtests were NOT arXiv-derived (ORB from user's topic list, ATR-brk from exp-005, MIM from memory). Actually read 2 directly-relevant arXiv papers on MNQ (=Micro Nasdaq, our underlying), both Mathias Mesfin 2026: (1) "Structural Limits of OHLCV-Based Intraday Signals in MNQ" (arXiv 2605.04004) — 14 OHLCV signal families, 5min 2021-25, strict bar (walk-forward, t>2, >=30 trades, net-positive after 2pt friction, cross-year) -> NONE pass, gross 0.07-1.50pt/trade < 2pt cost; (2) "VVG Classifier" (2605.11423) — gap+first30min+first-bar-vol regime, "morning continuation then late reversal" but not a robust standalone signal. | Applied Mesfin's falsification bar to OUR NAS100 ATR-breakout brick (real M1): passes n(812)/t(3.00)/net(+0.13R) BUT cross-year WEAK (2018,2019 neg, 7/9) AND selection-sensitive -> regime-filter OFF t=1.69 (fail), long-only t=1.75 (fail). Only the selected regime=low+both config clears t>2. Independent same-instrument study says intraday Nasdaq OHLCV edges don't survive -> our PF 1.27 is plausibly partly regime-selection/multiple-testing. DOWNGRADE confidence: brick is borderline, needs forward-test, not "confirmed strong". Lesson: run the research->backtest loop for real (implement a paper's rule / use its validation criteria), don't just scrape.
## [2026-07-28] research+experiment | Systematic 1000-paper arXiv search for a 2nd brick (user directive). Pulled 1000 q-fin papers (edgelab.research.fetch_corpus, 2026-01..07), TRIAGED by family+backtestability (edgelab/research/triage.py): only 178/1000 implementable on our OHLCV data (excluded crypto 197, NLP 192, fundamentals 123, options 96, theory 78, LOB 73). Backtestable families: cross-sectional 49 (dead), regime 46 (meta-method), momentum 30 (=brick1), volatility 17, breakout 11 (=brick1), seasonality 6, reversal 6, pairs 1. Built a VALIDATION GATE (edgelab/validation.py = Mesfin arXiv 2605.04004 criteria: t>2, net-of-cost, cross-year, +|corr|<0.3 vs brick1). Batteried the under-explored decorrelated families. | RESULT — FOUND A 2ND BRICK: **XAU turn-of-month** (long gold last trading day + first 2, protective -1.5% stop). Gate: t=3.77, mean +0.55%/mo, 10/10 years positive, **corr -0.00 to NAS brick**. Robust plateau across first-N window (t 2.28-2.34 w/o stop, 3.77 w/ stop). Turn-of-month is an a-priori documented anomaly -> softens multiple-testing (was 2/54 nominal calendar passes = ~chance, but ToM survives robustness + is pre-registered). PAIRS/cointegration family ALL DEAD (gold-silver/index/gold-crude neg, cost kills reversion). 2-BRICK PORTFOLIO (NAS ATR-brk + XAU ToM, equal-risk): Sharpe 0.96 -> **1.47** (+0.51), CAGR +9.8%, maxDD 6.0%, prop PASSED. Breadth engine finally turns 1->2. Caveats: monthly/low-activity diversifier, needs forward-test, Pepperstone-demo data. Tools: edgelab/edges/turn_of_month.py, validation.py, triage.py, research/_out/corpus/. FIRST credible decorrelated 2nd brick in the project.

## [2026-07-28] correction+experiment | HONEST self-check: my first brick#4 reversal pass did NOT implement the papers' actual signal (used generic z-score/RSI2 buy-the-dip, and mis-attributed 2607.01550 which is about TREND-following not reversal). Re-did it faithfully: implemented arXiv 2606.29591's exact signal (position=−sign(return[t−lag])) and VERIFIED its claims on our data. Findings: indices lag-1 reversal GROSS t=3.62 but NET t=1.12 (dies on cost = the BID-ASK BOUNCE the paper says is microstructure/untradable); lag-4 gross 3.28→net 0.93; lag-3 (paper's significant lag on 1993-2026) ≈0 on our 2016-2026 (effect decayed); FX/crypto random walk at all lags. So we REPRODUCED the paper's core result (lag-1 non-tradable bounce, reversion equities-only) and confirmed no tradable brick#4 net of cost. Lesson recorded: implement a paper's SPECIFIC signal + check its stated caveats (here: cost kills the microstructure lag-1), don't substitute a generic textbook proxy. Tool: scratchpad acf/lag-reversal test.

US+EU indices M10. First pass with SL/TP gave momentum t=-6 / fade t=-5 (BOTH lose) -> traced to the SL/TP barrier structure (pessimistic ties + TP>SL) dragging a near-zero-edge series. This VERIFIES the VVG paper's own conclusion ("does not translate into a robust standalone signal under realistic execution"). ## [2026-07-28] CRITICAL CORRECTION | Audited the OPR brick#4 backtest (user asked re: tz/execution). TIMEZONE = CORRECT (verified: NAS100 M15 tick-volume peaks exactly at 09:30 NY, drops after 16:00 NY, DST handled: 09:30NY=14:30UTC winter/13:30 summer). BUT EXECUTION BUG FOUND: my limit-fill logic was OPTIMISTIC — on an M15 bar whose range spans BOTH the pullback-limit AND the +1R cancel/runaway level, I assumed FILL. Under PESSIMISTIC fill (if the bar hit the runaway level, assume NOT filled), the entire edge vanishes: NAS breakouts/BE/SL1.5 t=+1.50 (optimistic) -> **t=-0.15 (pessimistic, -4R)**; +1-2pt slippage -> -0.27/-0.41; 4-asset breakouts optimistic t=+0.96 -> pessimistic **t=-0.84**. So the prior "best config t=1.50" was an OPTIMISTIC-FILL ARTIFACT (removing ~13% ambiguous fills kills it). M15 is too coarse to resolve limit-fill-vs-cancel honestly (needs M1/tick on the entry window). RETRACTED: OPR brick#4 has NO robust edge under honest execution; even the optimistic case was sub-threshold (t=1.5). Lesson: limit-order + cancel-if-runaway strategies MUST be resolved on finer data than the signal timeframe, else the ambiguous-bar fill assumption manufactures the edge. Tool: scratchpad/brick4_opr2.py (pess_fill + entry_slip params added).

Pulled US30/US500/GER40 M1. RESULT lands BETWEEN the optimistic-M15 (t1.5) and pessimistic-M15 (t-0.15): best = NAS breakouts/no-BE/hold-target **t=1.31** (E[R]+0.078 PF1.12 maxDD20R), NAS breakouts/BE/SL1.5 t=1.07, 4-asset breakouts t=0.99, full-spec(both) t=0.25, SL1.0 t=-2.19 (too tight, LOSES), SL2.0 t=0.19. Still SUB-THRESHOLD (t<2) -> NOT a deployable brick#4. Definitive verdict: real but weak edge (NAS opening breakout, different execution), plateaus ~t1.3. SL=1.5xATR optimal (1.0 loses). Tool: scratchpad/brick4_m1.py; US30/US500/GER40 M1 cached.

## [2026-07-29] BUGFIX | User caught MC (~+29 R/yr) vs backtest (+37.9). Root cause = TWO stacked bugs in the Monte-Carlo daily-R series, both understating: (1) used the COMPOUNDING engine mark-to-market (res.returns/rpt) for the crypto brick instead of fixed-fractional TRADE-R (compounding distorts the R total on the high-growth crypto sleeve, +14.7 vs +24 R/yr); (2) reindexed on business days (freq='B') which silently DROPPED crypto WEEKEND exits (crypto trades 7d/7). Fixed = fixed-fractional trade-R on a CALENDAR index (freq='D'). Corrected MC median +37.6 now MATCHES the backtest +37.9 (the correctness check for a block-bootstrap). Both prior MC scripts (montecarlo_R.py, mc_challenge_funded.py) had this bug -> superseded. Lesson: a bootstrap median that doesn't reproduce the historical mean is a red flag; and freq='B' is wrong for any 7-day-a-week asset.

FUNDED (-10% static, monthly payout resets to initial): optimal 0.5% (20%/yr, 2.4% ruin) or 0.75% (29%/yr, 15% ruin); E[withdrawn] plateaus 40-43% past 1.25% while ruin explodes -> NEVER >1%; asymmetric plan = pass at 1.0%, drop to 0.5% funded. PAYOUT CADENCE: biweekly NOT better -> income ~flat across cadence but frequent payout strips the cushion (reset-to-100% = always 10% from static floor) -> ~2x ruin vs quarterly; take payouts infrequently unless counterparty risk; +4% buffer (if partial withdrawals allowed) halves ruin for -3pts income. ## [2026-07-29] build | Made the live runner run-forever robust (user: "fais ce qui est mieux"). (5) install_task.ps1 — registers a Scheduled Task 'edgelab-live' to auto-start the supervisor at logon (no admin). SL/TP are broker-side so risk stays capped even if the runner is down; only time-exits + new entries need it alive. Note headless restart needs the MT5 terminal to auto-login (leave running or set mt5_path + save credentials). Best = VPS. Compile + PS1 syntax verified. Updated README + codebase-map.

## [2026-07-31] correction+analysis | Re-examined GER40 high-vol/both after user pushback ("why is M10 a problem for an M1 intraday strat?"). RESULT: my two robustness rejections were BOTH INVALID and are WITHDRAWN. (1) M1→M10: not a valid test — M1 is the native fill-EXACT resolution: 0/494 trades have an M1 bar straddling both stop&TP, so pessimistic(stop-first)==optimistic(tp-first)==t2.28 (no bar-method over-count); M10's lower t is an artifact of coarser bars mis-firing the pessimistic tie-rule. (2) recent-half "decay": NOT significant — early E[R] 0.20 vs recent 0.10, bootstrap 95% CI of the diff [−0.15,+0.35] includes 0; full-sample bootstrap P(E[R]≤0)=0.009. So GER40 high-vol/both is a GENUINE decorrelated validated edge (t=2.28, fill-exact, robust to regime-threshold 2.2-2.7 & session 2.07-2.29 & cost 2.11-2.28), same tier as brick1 (both = thin unconditional breakout t~1.6 lifted by a fitted vol-regime filter; NAS off1.66→low2.80, GER40 off1.63→high2.28). BUT — user's portfolio question settled it: at EQUAL maxDD, adding GER40 gives only +4% R/yr (32.5→33.8); it's the LOWEST-Sharpe sleeve (1.63 vs gold3.81/crypto2.73/NAS1.71) and adding it LOWERS combined Sharpe 2.42→2.37 (RoMaD barely moves 2.06→2.14). A 4th below-average decorrelated sleeve = diminishing returns; +4% doesn't justify its regime-selection model risk + operational complexity. DECISION: GER40 recorded as a validated decorrelated edge but NOT added; 3-brick book stands for risk-adjusted (not robustness) reasons. Ledger GER40 row + ⭐ near-miss note corrected accordingly. Tools: scratchpad/dax_orb.py, dax_robust.py, channel_breakout.py, brick_baseline.py.
## [2026-07-31] live-fix | Live runner spammed ~180 identical tracebacks over ~1h (23:00→00:01 UTC, last trading day of July). Cause: GoldTomStrategy (brick2 turn-of-month) fires XAUUSD long at the daily rollover, but gold was in its daily maintenance break → order_send retcode 10018 "Market closed". broker.place_market raised a bare RuntimeError; one_pass caught it with LOG.exception (full traceback) every ~20s pass, and since strat._acted_day is set only AFTER a successful place_market, it retried until the market reopened. It DID self-resolve: entered at 00:01:14 UTC (XAUUSD LONG 0.01 @4105.01 ticket 81681843) ~1h "late" at the reopen price. Nothing crashed; supervisor+runner stayed alive. FIX (commit 3a1fb91, pushed origin/main): new `MarketClosed(RuntimeError)` in broker.py raised on retcode 10018 (both entry + exit paths); one_pass catches it separately → logs ONE info line going in, retries silently, logs ONE info line ("market reopened, order placed, waited …") on fill. Retry-until-open behaviour unchanged, only the log spam removed. User chose "retry silencieux" over cap-late-entry/skip. Deploys to the live box on next restart (git reset --hard origin/main). Tools: edgelab/live/broker.py, edgelab/live/runner.py.

## [2026-07-31] live-deploy | BRICK 4 (IBS reversion) WIRED INTO THE LIVE RUNNER (user: "je veux que la brique 4 soit déployée en live sur le serveur"). New `NasIbsStrategy` (magic **105**) + `signals.ibs_state()` + `verify.verify_brick4()`; registered in `runner.build()` behind `enable_ibs` (default true). Design: decides ONCE per broker day at the D1 rollover (like bricks 2-3 = where the backtest fills, the next bar's open); long-only, **no TP**; exits = broker-managed stop / `IBS>0.8` at a close / 30 D1 **bars** — bars counted by locating the entry bar in the broker's own D1 frame, NOT calendar days (NAS100 prints none at weekends, a calendar count would time-exit ~2 days early). Shares NAS100 with brick 1 but a separate magic => separate positions, no interference. Reuses `edges/ibs.py`'s own `_wilder_atr` so the indicator math cannot fork; reproduces the frozen rule's DOUBLE ATR lag on purpose (run_ibs's `atr` is already `.shift(1)`, so 1R uses ATR through bar t-2 — deliberately not "corrected"). Waits for the day's bar to actually print before entering, so the stop is never sized off a stale close. NO config change needed to deploy (`ibs_symbol`/`enable_ibs` default to NAS100/true; config_live.yaml is gitignored). | VERIFY: brick4 signal math **314/314 trades exact** vs `run_ibs` (entry, exit, reason, R) — the strictest parity check of the four bricks (bricks 1-3 compare entries only); brick1 830/830, brick2 124/126, brick3 identical still PASS; 22/22 unit tests green; `runner --status` against the real PepperstoneUK-Demo shows NasIbsStrategy magic=105 flat alongside the 3 live positions. | ⚠️ FINDING: **the deployable brick is 0.8 R/yr thinner than its backtest, structurally.** `run_ibs`'s entry check runs AFTER its exit block in the same bar iteration, so it can re-enter on the SAME daily bar a stop fired, filling at that bar's **open** = a price already in the past. 13 such trades over 2018-07→2026-07. Live cadence: **287 trades, +4.81 R/yr, t=5.16, PF 2.21, 8/9 +yrs** vs backtest **300 trades, +5.64 R/yr, t=4.77, PF 1.99** (t and PF IMPROVE — the dropped re-entries were high-variance). ## [2026-07-31] correction+reports | BOOK RECUT ON THE LIVE CADENCE (user: "recoupe le backtest et le monte carlo sur la cadence live"). While implementing it I found my previous-turn attribution was WRONG and incomplete: the gap between the exploratory IBS loop and the deployed driver is not one artefact (same-bar re-entry) but **three**, all from the loop running its entry test AFTER the exit block of the same bar and testing the exit on the CURRENT bar instead of the last closed one: (a) it can re-enter on the very bar a stop fired, filling at that bar's OPEN = a price already in the past; (b) it NEVER tests the exit on the entry bar (it enters at the bottom of that iteration), so a signal exit needs >=2 bars while the driver, seeing the entry bar close at the next rollover, can exit after ONE; (c) it resolves the stop of bar t before considering an entry on bar t. My first fix (a `same_bar_reentry` flag) only covered (a) and produced 303 trades vs the driver's 302 with 269 mismatched rows -> discarded. REPLACED by an explicit `run_ibs(..., cadence=...)`: **"live" (new DEFAULT)** = one decision per daily-bar rollover on the last CLOSED bar, filling at the new bar's open = literally what `NasIbsStrategy.step` does; **"literal"** = the original exp-009 loop, kept ONLY so `verify` can prove the live signal math reproduces it (still 314/314 exact). Verified the live cadence reproduces the driver replay trade-for-trade. Also checked a 4th suspected difference — the driver `return`s after an exit so it cannot exit-and-enter in the same pass — and it costs **0 trades / 0.0 R** over the whole history (a 30-bar time-exit never coincides with IBS<0.2), so the driver was left unchanged. | RECUT BOOK (canonical, `build_reports.py`): IBS brick **+5.64 -> +4.81 R/yr** (n 300->287, t 4.77->**5.16**, PF 1.99->**2.21**, still 8/9 +yrs — the edge is STRONGER on the live cadence, only the R/yr was optimistic). Book **+38.2 -> +37.3 R/yr** (+301 R), maxDD 13.4->**13.8 R**, RoMaD 2.85->2.71, Sharpe 2.63->**2.59**, win days 53->54%, positive 9/9 yrs (2018 half-yr +2.1). Corr matrix IMPROVES: IBS vs NAS **−0.00**, gold −0.03, crypto −0.01 (max |corr| 0.03). MC 40k: median +37.6->**+36.8**, mean +37.2, P(profit yr) 98.2->**98.1%**, 5th-pct yr +7.7->**+7.1 R**, median maxDD 9.9 R, 99th 24.1. ⚠️ **Worst day −4.15 -> −4.43 R** (IBS shifts which day is worst) => the structural sizing ceiling TIGHTENS from ~1.2% to **~1.1%/trade**; challenge @1% 92.8->92.6% in 3.8mo with still 0% daily breaches; funded @0.5% 19.3%/yr at 1.9% ruin. Both HTML reports regenerated (data blobs + every hard-coded prose number), plus _out/ and root RAPPORT_*.html copies. verify 4/4 PASS, 22/22 tests green. Wiki reconciled: system.md (brick table, corr, combined table, MC table, prop sizing, caveat 2b rewritten), exp-009 (results 4-5 + caveat 4 rewritten with the 3 real causes + a note that results 1-3 were run on the literal loop), codebase-map, live/README. Lesson: a backtest loop's ITERATION ORDER is part of the strategy — wiring a brick to a live driver is itself a validation step that a backtest alone cannot provide.

(4) **DIAGNOSIS — the part that matters: GROSS of cost there is NO edge in either direction.** Gross t spans −1.74 to **+1.81** across the 7 pairs (insignificant everywhere) while net t spans −0.95 to −5.91: the entire loss is friction (~0.08 R/trade at ~230 trades/yr/pair). And the MIRROR (fading the breakout) is negative too, t −1.92 to −4.56, because it pays the same spread. The Asian-range breakout is neither a trend nor a reversion edge — a coin flip with a toll. Same wall as SmartFX/Temnov/Donchian-FX/regime-MR, and Mesfin 2026's "gross edge ceiling < friction", now measured on NATIVE M1 rather than inferred. QS screen final tally: 338 articles -> 0 new brick. Tools: scratchpad/london_breakout.py, pull_m1_london.py, RESEARCH_LOG_QS.md.
## [2026-07-31] validation | IBS US500 as a 5th brick — REJECTED on decorrelation (user: "regarde si IBS US500 comme 5eme brique est decorrele et ameliore le portefeuille"). It is a GENUINE edge: n=273, +3.3 R/yr, t=3.48, PF 1.74, 7/9 +yrs, bootstrap P(E[R]<=0)=0.0010, cost-robust to 6pt (t=2.03 — exp-009's "cost-fragile past 4pt" does not hold on the live cadence), and its split-half runs the RIGHT way (early t=1.50 / late t=2.83, i.e. improving, not decaying — also contra exp-009). It DOES improve the book: +2.3 pts/yr at equal risk, RoMaD 2.24->2.46, Sharpe 2.57->2.68, maxDD 14.3->14.2. | **BUT it fails the decorrelation half of the question, and that is the half exp-009 never tested**: exp-009 measured US500 IBS against the three bricks OF THE TIME, never against brick 4 — which is the same mechanism on a co-moving US index. **corr(IBS-US500, IBS-NAS100) = +0.574**, twenty times every existing brick-brick correlation (all <=0.03); vs the other three it is fine (+0.005 / -0.012 / -0.009). **69% of the candidate's active days close a trade on the same day as brick 4.** It is not a 5th independent bet, it is brick 4 at larger size with partial index diversification. | **DECISIVE TEST**: if it is really just scale, does scale do it better? Yes — **sizing brick 4 at 1.6R beats adding US500 at 1R**: +3.8 vs +2.3 pts/yr at equal risk, maxDD 13.3 vs 14.2 R, RoMaD 2.62 vs 2.46, Sharpe 2.75 vs 2.68. Root cause: brick 4's standalone RoMaD is **1.59** vs US500's **0.87** — adding a diluted copy of a bet you already hold is dominated by doing more of the better copy. (Sizing b4 up is a concentration/model-risk decision, not a new edge — recorded, not recommended.) A 5th brick still needs a DIFFERENT mechanism. Tools: scratchpad/validate_ibs_us500.py.
## [2026-07-31] research | quantocracy.com screened end-to-end (user: same filter as the QS pass). FUNNEL: **7606 links across 154 index pages and 409 source blogs** -> 1013 on an allowed asset -> 662 minus external-data/wrong-asset markers -> 254 rule-ish -> 231 fetched from 83 blogs -> 144 with usable text (87 dead, blocked or paywalled) -> **only 4 fully specified AND in scope**. Quantocracy is a link aggregator of quantitative RESEARCH, not of executable rules: **87% of its links are categories the user excluded** - single-stock factor research (Alpha Architect alone = 1076 links), asset allocation / risk parity / TAA, ML methodology, options & vol surface, fixed income, macro. Rejected at the read stage: DTR Trading (RUT iron condors = options), Alvarez (country-ETF rotation, dividend screens, Hi-Lo breadth), Black Arbs (2-asset allocation), Allocate Smartly (monthly TSM, ledger-killed), Rulyfi (meta-research on overfitting), Quantpedia MACD-on-BTC (= brick 3). | RESULTS, 0 new brick: **Q1** BTC hour-seasonality, verbatim "buy Bitcoin at 21:00 (UTC+0) and sell it at 23:00 (UTC+0)" -> **t=-7.19, PF 0.65, 3/9 +yrs**, whole neighbourhood (19->23, 20->22, 21->22) -5.3 to -13.1. **Q2** "short at 09:15 GMT Wed/Thu/Fri, close after 5 hours" -> **negative on all 7 majors** (t -0.14 EURUSD to -5.20 USDCAD); the article's own comment section objects it is "just the post-2008 EURUSD downtrend" - WRONG and informatively so: the **symmetric LONG loses too (t=-3.58)**, so both directions pay the spread, it is friction not trend; EURUSD split-half +0.71 then -0.77. **Q3** 10-day-high breakout -> ETH t=2.69 (+3.1 R/yr) but BTC t=0.87: one asset of two, sub-threshold, and the crypto-trend family brick 3 already covers. **Q4** BTC overnight after a local X-day high -> **t~2.07-2.23 across X in {10,20,30,50}, +1.3 to +1.7 R/yr** - real but smaller than brick 2 (+2.5), the thinnest sleeve, so below the bar. | WARNING - **THE FIND THAT MATTERS: a look-ahead that read t=17.43 and nearly shipped.** Q4 first came out at +11.8 R/yr, t=17.43, **PF 10.51**, 9/9 +years. Caught by the profit factor (an overnight crypto trade does not have PF 10). CAUSE: after `to_true_utc` a **D1 bar stamped day D covers D 21:00 UTC -> D+1 21:00 UTC**, so its CLOSE is only known 24h after its timestamp - but the "on a local X-day high" filter used that close while the entry was at 21:00 UTC on day D. The filter was selecting nights that ENDED at a high: it read the very move it claimed to trade. FIX: give every daily value an explicit `known_at = timestamp + 1 day` and require `known_at <= entry time` -> **t collapses 17.43 -> 2.23**. Calibration: the identical window with NO filter is -1.81 R/yr, t=-1.33 net of cost, so the raw overnight window does not even pay. LESSON (third variant this month, after the tz bug and the two cadence corrections): **on a broker feed a daily bar's timestamp is the START of its session, not a date** - any rule mixing a daily condition with an intraday entry needs an explicit known-at time or it reads the future. Tools: scratchpad/qc_index.py, qc_triage.py, qc_fetch.py, qc_rules.py, qc_run.py, RESEARCH_LOG_QUANTOCRACY.md.
## [2026-07-31] hardening | Live runner: a closed market with NO QUOTE is now typed as `MarketClosed` (user: "les briques qui tradent au rollover peuvent-elles faire crasher l'algo si le marche est ferme du vendredi au dimanche 23h ?"). AUDIT FIRST — the answer is no: `one_pass` wraps every `strat.step` in its own try/except so no brick can kill the loop, retcode 10018 is already typed as `MarketClosed` and logged once then retried quietly, and that path runs EVERY day (the rollover decision at 00:00 server falls in Pepperstone's daily maintenance break), so it is well-worn code, not an untested weekend edge case. Per brick over a weekend: **brick 2 never even tries** (`_business_tom_flags` is a business-day calendar, so `is_entry_day`/`is_exit_day` cannot be true on a Sat/Sun); **brick 4 entry never sends an order** (the `if not forming` guard waits for the day's bar to print so the stop is never sized off a stale close — it fills at the reopen, which IS the next bar's open = where the backtest fills); **brick 1** returns on an empty session mask; the only path that really hits the wall is a **brick 4 exit** (`IBS>0.8` on a Friday close), which retries until Monday and closes at the open, as the backtest does. | THE ONE REAL DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED: `place_market`/`close` did `tick.ask`/`tick.bid` unguarded. A symbol in its break does not always reject with 10018 — MT5 can return `None` (or a 0.0 bid/ask) and the order is never sent, which surfaced as an **AttributeError**, i.e. the generic branch, i.e. a full traceback every 20 s for up to 48 h (~8 600 of them). Not a crash (the log is capped at 5 MB x 5) but it would rotate the useful history out of `runner.log`. New `Broker._tick_price(sym, want_ask, mt5)` raises `MarketClosed` on a missing/zero quote and is used by `place_market`, `close` and the requote-refresh inside `_send_retry` (same unguarded `tick.ask` there). Behaviour is unchanged when the market is open. | NOT DONE IN THIS PASS (the cutoff was added right after — see the next entry): a staleness cutoff on the daily bricks (bricks 2/3/4 have no equivalent of brick 1's "stale breakout -> skip today" guard, so a non-10018 failure such as invalid stops 10016 or AutoTrading-off 10027 retries all day and could fill hours after the rollover = not the backtest's trade), and keying `_MARKET_CLOSED_SINCE` by (name, magic) since BTC and ETH share the class name `CryptoMacdStrategy`. `edgelab/live/broker.py`.
## [2026-07-31] hardening | ENTRY RETRY CUTOFF on the rollover bricks — and the user caught the design flaw in my first proposal. I had offered "past X hours after the rollover without a fill, skip the day"; the user objected: *"si un signal est valide a la cloture de vendredi il faut que le trade soit pris a la reouverture dimanche 23h, il ne faudrait pas que la regle soit appliquee a ce moment la"*. He is right and it inverts the anchor — a wall-clock cutoff measured from the rollover would kill EVERY Friday-close signal, because the legitimate wait to the Sunday-night reopen is ~48 h and that reopen IS where the backtest fills (the next bar's open). **The cutoff must be measured from the reopen, not from the rollover.** | IMPLEMENTATION (`edgelab/live/strategies.py`): new `_EntryRetryGuard` + `_RolloverBrick._place_entry`, mixed into bricks 2, 3 and 4 (brick 1 already has its own "stale breakout -> skip today" guard). The clock is started by **failures on an OPEN market only**: `MarketClosed` (retcode 10018) is re-raised untouched, and brick 4's "today's bar has not printed yet" path never reaches the guard at all — so a shut market costs nothing and the weekend wait stays deliberately UNBOUNDED. What is bounded is a tradable market that keeps refusing us (invalid stops 10016, AutoTrading off 10027, a mis-mapped symbol), where the retry used to run all day at one attempt/20 s and could land a fill hours from the rollover = not the trade the backtest measured. Past `entry_retry_max_hours` (new config key, default **2**) the brick logs an ERROR and sets `_acted_day`, skipping the day. **EXITS are never bounded** — you do not abandon closing a position. | VERIFIED by simulation over a real Fri-22:00-UTC -> Sun-22:00-UTC window at the true 20 s poll and the true Athens broker day: (1) weekend = 145 rejected attempts then **FILLED at the Sunday 23h-Paris reopen, day never skipped**; (2) open market rejecting = day skipped after **2.0056 h**; (3) weekend THEN a rejecting open market = skipped **2 h after the REOPEN**, not 2 h after Friday — the anchor is right. (A first run showed 4.3 h in case 2: my harness was keying the broker day on the UTC date, which flips 2 h into the scenario and restarted the clock; with the real Athens date it is exactly 2.0 h. Test artefact, not a code path.) | REGRESSION: `verify` 4/4 PASS unchanged (brick1 830/830, brick2 124/126 same two Good Fridays, brick3 cadence 0 same-bar re-opens, brick4 314/314), 22/22 tests green. LESSON: a timeout on a trading retry needs its anchor tied to *market availability*, not to wall clock — the same class of mistake as counting brick 4's hold in calendar days instead of D1 bars.

## [2026-07-31] tooling | DAILY DISCORD REPORT rebuilt as a rich EMBED (user: "c'est un rapport pas tres aesthetique"). The 23h-Paris heartbeat was 4 lines of plain text in a ``` block; it is now a coloured Discord embed (`summary.build_report_embed`) — green/red/grey by the day's realised R, three inline KPI fields (Account / Today / Since start), a **challenge progress bar** (block-glyph bar of the balance between the −10% static floor and the +15% target, the number that actually matters on a prop account), open positions one per line with a direction dot and the owning brick, and a monospace per-brick table with PF / avg / best / worst. Footer carries the ET timestamp + the running commit + the server, so the heartbeat now also tells you WHICH build is live. `send_discord` gained an `embed=` parameter (plain-text path unchanged); the runner falls back to the old text report if the embed POST fails, since the heartbeat matters more than the formatting. New `python -m edgelab.live.summary --preview` prints the embed JSON without sending (and reconfigures stdout to utf-8 — the Windows console is cp1252 and cannot print the emoji). | TWO CORRECTNESS FIXES the redesign surfaced, both about the report telling the truth: (1) **open positions now come from the BROKER, not from trades.csv.** The local journal has 2 rows but the account holds 3 positions — a position opened while `trade_log_csv` was unset is absent from the journal FOREVER, so a CSV-derived "open" list can silently under-report what is at risk. `_maybe_report` now takes `strategies` and reads `broker.open_position(magic)` per brick. (2) **per-brick attribution was wrong for NAS100**, which brick 1 and brick 4 both trade under their own magic: the journal's ENTRY rows name the brick (comment "brick4_ibs_reversion") but its EXIT rows carried only "stop"/"take"/"time_exit"/"ibs_high", so every closed NAS100 trade was charged to brick 1 by the symbol->brick fallback map. `Broker._tag` now prefixes the journaled exit reason with the owning brick ("brick4:ibs_high") and `summary._brick(symbol, reason)` reads it — exact attribution with NO CSV schema change (the field is free text; legacy rows just fall back to the map). Verified on a synthetic journal containing one brick-1 and two brick-4 NAS100 trades: they now split correctly (b1 n=1 −1.00 R, b4 n=2 +1.92 R) where before all three read as brick 1. | verify 4/4 PASS, 22/22 tests green. `edgelab/live/summary.py`, `runner.py`, `broker.py`.

## [2026-08-03] experiment | **Full validation battery on the Pine candidate (RobBooker_ADX, NAS100 H1) — ⛔ REJECTED, and the earlier "+29 %/yr, strongest candidate ever" read is WITHDRAWN as over-claimed** (user: "fais les tests nécessaires pour valider ce candidat sur NAS100"). Six-part battery: long-only-beta null, parameter neighbourhood, fill realism, decay significance, path risk, look-ahead; then a block-bootstrap portfolio re-test. | **WHAT PASSED (and is worth keeping on record):** parameter neighbourhood is genuinely good — **150 points (lookback × ADX threshold × TP/SL multiples), 100% positive, median t +1.88, 45% above 2, the published point ranks 6/150** so it is NOT a fitted peak (compare GER40: median 1.43, 11% above 2); immediate neighbours median t=2.71. **Fills are essentially EXACT on H1** — only **1 of 364** trades resolves on a bar containing both barriers (0.3%), pessimistic t=3.18 vs optimistic 3.28, so there is no bar-method optimism (cf. the GER40 M1 fill-exactness note). **No look-ahead**: delaying the fill one extra bar gives t=2.77 — graceful decay, not a collapse. **No outlier dependence**: the payoff is capped at +2R/−1R so there are no fat tails; trimming both tails 5% → t=2.71 (the scary-looking "drop best 10% → t=0.60" is mechanical — it removes 40% of all winners from a fixed bracket). **Short side also positive** (t=1.17, PF 1.18) → not pure long beta. Split-half drop 2.86→1.62 is NOT significant on its own (bootstrap CI of the difference [−0.153,+0.440] includes 0). | **WHAT KILLED IT. (a) THE NULL IS NOT ZERO — the decisive finding and a new general lesson.** A long-only 1.0×/0.5× box bracket on a drifting index earns **t ≈ +0.94 with RANDOM entry bars** (400 draws, 95% of them reach +2.84). So the `bootstrap P(E[R]≤0)=0.0002` reported earlier tested the WRONG hypothesis. Against the correct null — same bracket geometry, same trade count, random entries — the honest significance is **p = 0.017**; and with **283 tests in the sweep, ~4.8 cells are expected at p≤0.017 by chance**, so the best cell of 283 having p=0.017 is precisely what noise produces. **(b) The recent half is insignificant**: second-half **p=0.115** vs the null (first half 0.025). **(c) Walk-forward fails**: best params fitted on the first half (lookback 25, ADX<18, t=3.23) score **t=1.04** on the second. **(d) Honest RoMaD is 0.79, not 1.10** — the realised maxDD of 9.2R was a lucky ordering, the shuffle median is **13.6R** (95th 21.8R), which demotes it from "2nd of five sleeves" to *middling*: IBS 1.59 > crypto 0.95 > **cand 0.79 = NAS 0.79** > gold 0.73. **(e) Bear-year evidence is thin**: 2022 = +1.4R over 37 trades, E[R]=+0.038 ≈ flat, not the robustness it first appeared to be. **PORTFOLIO re-test on 3000 block-bootstrapped paths (5-day blocks) instead of the single realised path: adding it = +4.8 pts/yr median with P(beats current book)=73%; swapping gold out = +3.2 pts/yr, P=67%** — far below the +6.7/+7.0 single-path figures, and that already ASSUMES the edge is real and stationary, which (b)/(c) deny. **VERDICT: probably a real but very weak edge, statistically indistinguishable from the best of 283 noise draws, with an insignificant recent half. NOT a brick, NOT a replacement. The 4-brick book stands unchanged.** | **NEW STANDING LESSON: for a long-only capped-bracket system on a drifting instrument, `bootstrap P(E[R]≤0)` is the WRONG null — it tests against zero when the geometry alone pays. Always calibrate against RANDOM ENTRIES with the same bracket and holding distribution.** This is the fourth variant of the project's recurring "wrong baseline" error (after the tz bug, the two cadence corrections, and the daily-condition look-ahead). scratchpad/tv_nas_battery.py, tv_nas_battery2.py, tv_nas_portfolio_mc.py; mt4_backtest.py gained a PESSIMISTIC fill switch
## [2026-08-03] experiment | **ALL SEVEN Pine cells at t>2 re-tested against the random-entry null — 0 of 7 survive** (user: "il n'y a aucun autre candidat parmi les 7 pine scripts testables ?" — a fair challenge, the full battery had only been run on the top cell). Built a GENERIC geometry null that works for any bracket shape (ATR, box-width or fixed-tick): take the strategy's OWN sl/tp arrays, draw the same number of entry bars uniformly at random from the bars where that bracket is defined, use the bracket THAT bar would have had, sample direction from the strategy's realised long/short mix, resolve with the same pessimistic engine and the same cost; 400 draws → a null t-distribution. Criteria for a candidate: p<0.05 vs null on the whole sample AND on the recent half, t>2 at +10 pts/side, honest (shuffled-maxDD) RoMaD>0.9, some cross-asset support. | **RESULTS (t / n / p_whole / p_recent / t@+10pts / RoMaD / OOS median):** RobBooker_ADX·NAS100·H1 3.18/375/0.005/**0.124**/2.77/0.78/−0.25 · ForexMaster_v4·FRA40·H1 2.60/755/0.005/**0.000**/**1.56**/0.54/**−2.02** · ForexMaster_RSI·US2000·D1 2.38/**42**/0.015/0.004/2.11/0.67/−0.28 · Crypto3EMA·US500·H1 2.11/328/0.022/0.132/**0.81**/0.49/−0.22 · Crypto3EMA·NAS100·H1 2.06/404/0.035/0.052/1.62/0.40/−0.22 · RobBooker_ADX·USDJPY·H1 2.68/388/**0.057**/0.304/1.46/0.59/−0.25 · ZeroLagMA·EURUSD·H1 2.07/397/**0.085**/0.448/1.11/0.41/−0.37. **Two cells are MORE interesting than the one first chased, and both still fail. (1) ForexMaster_v4 on FRA40** — BB(20,1.5) fade + ADX filter — is the ONLY cell whose RECENT half is strongly significant (**split-half IMPROVES 0.61→3.16, p_recent=0.000**), the right direction for a live candidate; but it **dies on cost** (t 2.60→1.56 at +10 pts/side, and FRA40 is a wide-spread name — cf. the intraday cross-section row's 27-50bps half-spreads) and is **actively negative on 17 other assets** (median −2.02, worst OOS of the seven) → a single-market artefact on the most expensive index in the universe. **(2) ForexMaster_RSI on US2000 D1** clears THREE of four — p_whole 0.015, p_recent 0.004, cost-robust t=2.11 — and fails only on **size**: n=42 over 7.5y (5.6 trades/yr), **+2.1 R/yr, RoMaD 0.67**, i.e. thinner AND worse-pathed than gold (+2.5 R/yr, 0.73), already the book's weakest sleeve; adding it would dilute, not diversify. **DECISIVE FRAME — multiple testing: across the sweep's 283 tests, chance alone delivers ~14 cells at p<0.05 against the geometry null; only 5 of the 7 hits reach it. The Pine corpus produces FEWER geometry-beating cells than chance**, the same verdict as the MQL4 corpus but reached through a stricter null. **0 candidates; the 4-brick book stands.** Also confirms the new standing lesson generalises beyond long-only: the null t for these brackets ranges from −0.81 (ForexMaster_v4, a both-sided fade) to +1.04 (RobBooker, long-only) — the geometry's contribution is strategy-specific and must be measured per cell, never assumed to be 0. scratchpad/tv_null_all7.py

## [2026-08-03] research | TradingView SCRAPED at scale (5759 Pine strategies) + a Pine→Python TRANSPILER — and the two findings that outlive the null result. User: "procure-toi des milliers de pine scripts (scrappe), filtre (SL/TP/entrée clairs, purement OHLCV, or/forex/crypto/indices US-EU), convertis en Python et backteste à la lettre sur Pepperstone; re-teste les familles déjà vues". (1) **SCRAPE**: `pubscripts-suggest-json` enumerates published scripts 50/page with `extra.kind`, likes and access — ~49% of hits are strategies, offset ceiling 1000/term so coverage comes from term BREADTH, and the ~92% that don't inline their source come from `pine-facade/get/<id>/last`, which returned **5759/5759 (100%)**. A round-2 crawl-expansion (every word of every harvested title as a new term) added only **+453 (+8.5%) → saturation at ~5.8k**. Nine minutes of scraping, 2.4× the previous 2380-script dump. (2) **THE REAL CONSTRAINT WAS PORTING, NOT CORPUS SIZE** — the previous pass hand-ported 12 of 333 finalists. So this pass compiles Pine instead: `tv_pine.py` (tokenizer + precedence-climbing expression parser, vectorised `ta.*` runtime keeping Pine's own conventions) and `tv_transpile.py` (line joining, `if`/`else` blocks, `var` persistence, single- AND multi-line `=>` functions compiled to real Python defs). The user's filters became STRUCTURAL rather than textual: a rule that compiles necessarily has an entry + hard SL + hard TP, `request.*`/`security()` cannot compile at all (the no-external-data gate), and traded-volume dependence is now decided on the GENERATED code so a cosmetic `plot(volume)` no longer disqualifies a script the way a text match did. Three approximations stated and one-directional; the sharpest is that **`strategy.position_size` is resolved per COMPARISON, not to one number** — `== 0` ("am I flat?", which is also what freezes a breakout box) stays true while `> 0`/`< 0` answer the side being priced, so the body is evaluated three times. Collapsing it to a single value silently zeroes brackets — found by the correctness gate, not by reading. **VALIDATION vs the 12 hand ports: 3 reproduce at 100.000% bar-by-bar agreement, identical t, SL diff 0.000. The 4th divergence is the HAND PORT's error** — the published Pine reads `ta.cross` (both directions) with both sides enabled, while the hand port coded RobBooker_ADX long-only with `crossover`; that was the previous pass's headline candidate. Funnel **5759 → 375 distinct compiled rules** (31× the hand-port sample): 1150 lack a hard SL+TP, 584 grid/pyramiding, 427 external data, 158 repainting, 61 volume, ~1300 lost to transpiler limits (recursive `var`, `for` loops, pending entries — enumerated, not hidden). (3) **RESULT: 6948 cells, 5656 at n≥30 — 148 at t>2 against 130.1 expected by chance (1.14×), 13 at t>2.9 against 10.7; median t −0.66, 33% positive E[R]. At chance.** Battery on the top 20: **0 survive**. Closest is `Manadi EMA+MACD+RSI`/US30/D1 (p≤0.003 vs its own random-entry null, p_recent 0.005, cost-robust t=2.85, honest RoMaD 0.86, beating gold 0.73 and NAS 0.79 on path) — **not promotable**: at p≈0.003 over 5656 cells chance delivers ~19 such cells, and it carries 57 trades (~7/yr) for +4.3 R/yr with OOS +0.38. Named here so it is not re-discovered as new. (4) **FINDING 1 — THE GEOMETRY BASELINE, measured** (`tv_geometry.py`): entries drawn uniformly at RANDOM, 1×ATR stop / 2×ATR target, same engine and cost, 400 draws per asset×tf. **A long-only random bracket on XAUUSD D1 earns t=+2.07 with no rule at all** (NAS100 +1.45, US500 +1.58, USDJPY +1.28; USDCHF ≈+0.25) — the nominal t>2 bar is cleared by NOTHING. The both-directions null is ≈0 everywhere on D1, so the premium is purely directional drift collected by a long-biased bracket, and published retail Pine is overwhelmingly long-biased. Across 38 (asset×tf) pairs the baseline correlates **r=+0.485** with where the corpus's hits land — a large confound, not a full explanation (R²≈0.24). It **explains the corpus's most seductive result**: XAUUSD produced 45 t>2 cells against 7.2 expected (6.3×), and the gold cells with the HIGHEST t have the WORST p (`BitCoin Simple Buyer`/XAU/D1 reads t=+3.47 and is beaten by its own null, p=0.53). Standing rule made concrete: **look up an asset's geometry baseline before reading any cell — on gold and the US indices the bar is ≈+1.5 to +2.1, not 0.** (5) **FINDING 2 — H1 IS BELOW CHANCE, D1 IS NOT**: same rules, same assets, same engine, only the bar size changes — **D1 86 hits vs 46.9 expected (1.84×), median t −0.04, 48.4% positive E[R]; H1 62 vs 83.2 (0.74×), median t −1.12, 24.3% positive**. The geometry table gives the mechanism: on H1 the signal-free null is negative on all 19 assets (cost charged against a smaller ATR). The project's "gross edge ceiling < friction" reproduced as a distributional shift across thousands of independent cells instead of argued from a handful. (6) Families: the 375 rules are again recombinations of the same canon (MA crossovers, RSI/Stoch thresholds, Bollinger/Keltner, ATR brackets, Supertrend, Donchian, MACD, Ichimoku) — all already in the ledger — and IBS, the only signal that ever produced a brick here, is again essentially absent. CUMULATIVE: ~19800 papers/posts + 1046 GitHub files + 2201 MQL5 + 1256 MQL4 + 5759 Pine → **0 new brick**. The 4-brick book stands. `RESEARCH_LOG_TV2.md`.

## [2026-08-03] correction | Second user pushback — *"pourquoi ne pas garder GER40 ?"* — and on the reason I first gave, **he was right and I was close to a double standard.** I rejected `HITESH SOMANI`/GER40/D1 on CONCENTRATION (best trade 39% of P&L, top-3 58%).
